It broadcasts its presence, and that is picked up by our Windows laptop and others within the access point’s Radio Frequency (RF) range. It is important to note that in Open mode, which is the least secure, we configured our access point. For the time being, it is best not to connect this access point to the internet because someone inside the RF range may use it to access the internet.Have a go hero-set up the access point to use WEP and WPA Play around with your access point configuration options. Try using encryption schemes such as WEP and WPA / WPA2, to get it up and running. In later chapters, we will use those modes to illustrate attacks against them.Setting up the wireless cardIt’s much easier to set up our Wireless Adapter than the access point. The benefit is that Kali supports this out-of-the-box card and ships with the necessary system drivers to allow for packet injection and sniffing.Action time-setting up your wireless cardWith the penetration tester laptop, we will use the wireless adapter. To set up your card, please follow these step-by-step instructions:1\. Plugin the card to and boot one of the USB ports of the Kali laptop, Open a console terminal once you log in, and type in iwconfig. Your screen should look as follows: Wlan0 is the wireless interface created for the wireless adapter, as you can see. Type wlan0 into ifconfig to bring up the interface. Then type wlan0 in ifconfig to see the interface’s current state:2\. The MAC address 00: c0: ca:3e: bd:93 should match your Alfa card’s MAC address written under. I use the Edimax, which gives me the previous MAC address 80:1f:02:8f:34: d5\. This is a quick check to make sure you have the correct interface enabled.What happened just now?The Kali ships out of the box with all of the drivers required for the Alfa and Edimax adapters, as soon as the machine booted, the adapter was recognized and was assigned the network interface wlan0\. Our Wireless Adapter is now up and working!Connecting to the access pointNow we’ll take a look at how to use the wireless adapter to connect to the access point. Our access point is equipped with SSID Wireless Lab and uses no authentications.Time for action-setting up your wireless card Here we go!To connect your wireless card to the access point, follow these steps:1\. Let’s first see what our adapter is currently detecting on wireless networks. Issue the scanning command iwlist wlan0, and you’ll find a list of networks near you:![](image_rsrc8P3.jpg)Keep scrolling down, and this list should include the Wireless Lab network. It’s detected as Cell 05 in my setup; it could be different in yours. The field in ESSID contains the name of the network. 2\. Since multiple access points can have the same SSID, please verify that the MAC address mentioned in the previous Address field matches the MAC of your access point. Under the access point, or using web-based GUI settings, it is a quick and easy way to get the MAC address. 3\. Now issue the “Wireless Lab” command for iwconfig wlan0 essid, and then iwconfig wlan0 to check the status. If you have successfully connected to the access point, you should see the Access Point’s MAC address: field in the iwconfig output. 4\. We know from its manual that the access point has an IP address 192.168.0.1 for the management interface. Alternatively, when we run the route -n command, this is the same as the default router IP address. The ifconfig wlan0 192.168.0.2 netmask 255.255.255.255.0 up command lets us set our IP address in the same subnet. Verify the successful command by typing wlan0 ifconfig and checking the output. 5\. Now let’s ping out the access point by issuing the ping command 192.168.0.1\. If the network connection has been set up correctly, then you should see the access point responses. You can also issue an arp-a command to verify that the answer comes from the access point. You should see that the IP 192.168.0.1 MAC address is the MAC address of the access point, which we noted earlier. It is important to remember that some of the older access points may have disabled replies to the Internet Control Message Protocol (ICMP) echo request packets. This is usually achieved to provide out-of-the-box safe access point with only basic setup settings. In such a case, you can attempt to launch a browser and access the web interface to verify the up-and-run connection:![](image_rsrc8P4.jpg)We will check reliability on the access point by looking at the logs for the connections. As you can see in the following log, the wireless card’s MAC address 4C:0F:6E:70: BD: CB has been registered, allowing router DHCP requests:![](image_rsrc8P5.jpg)What just happened?We’ve just successfully linked from Kali to our access point using our wireless adapter as the wireless computer. We have also learned how to verify that both the wireless client and the access point side have established a connection. Have a go hero – set up a connection in a WEP setup.Creating the entry point in a WEP setup is a daunting practice for you. With both of these, consider using the wireless adapter to create a connection to the access point. Hint: search the iwconfig command manually by typing iwconfig man to see how the card can be programmed to connect to WEP.Chapter 9 : Linux Tasks and CommandsIn this chapter, we are going to go over the basic tasks that you can perform in Linux. We are going to show you how to install applications, how to update applications, how to look at the Linux task manager and even terminate processes if necessary, how to start services and a whole lot more. These are the basic tasks that you will need to understand in order to do anything else in Linux. Everything that will be shown here will be on the Ubuntu Linux server version. This is not the desktop version. You are going to be looking at the LUI, or the line user interface. You will be typing in commands on the keyboard instead of clicking a bunch of options in a graphical user interface. While this may seem a little daunting and tedious, keep in mind that in Linux, all types of serious administration have to be done at the command line. This is an operating system developed by programmers for programmers, primarily after all. Even if you are using the desktop version of Ubuntu Linux, it does not give you the full power of the operating system unless you use the terminal or command line to execute certain tasks. A lot of times, even if you are using a desktop version, you still have to open up a terminal screen and type out all the commands that you need to type out to administer the system.SudoThe first command we need to talk about before you start to do any of the other commands is “sudo.” Sudo basically means “superuser do.” We discussed in the previous chapters how different distributions do things slightly differently, and that each Linux distribution has its own quirks; its own little ways of doing things depending on what the creators are concerned about. Security was one of the issues Ubuntu Linux developers were concerned about. As we talked about before, there is a user named root in every Linux computer. The root is the computer’s highest level user. It is sort of like a Microsoft Windows device administrator. Just like on a Windows computer, if someone has logged in as the administrator, or someone has logged in as root on Linux, they can do whatever they want to do to that computer. They can install viruses, malware, or spyware, or just cause a lot of havoc, basically. Hackers will also attempt to login asroot using special programs and scripts, and create all these problems. To alleviate the risk of root access being stolen by a hacker, the Ubuntu developers agreed they would never allow someone to sign in directly asroot. But you can’t sign in as user root in Ubuntu Linux. Now the issue comes here. If you are unable to login as a user, then how do you do all those administrative tasks? How do you implement compliance processes? What they have is what they call this program sudo. It is basically a prefix of commands that tells the operating system you want to run a particular process like the superuser or root. Sudo temporarily gives administrative access to a user — root access — to execute a key command in Linux. In Windows operating system, sudo is the equivalent of the “Run as Administrator” option whenever you want to run a Windows-based program with administrative rights.Man PagesThe next thing we need to think about is pages of “kid.” Man-pages are man-pages. What you need to remember about man pages is that if you don’t understand how a command is supposed to work or what command you’re supposed to run, the man pages are where you’re looking for any command information. Let’s assume you want to browse details about the ping order, for example. What you do is type in man followed the order you’d like to find out. Look at the syntax below: $man ping. What this will do in Linux is it will open a page describing the ping command to you and everything you need to know about the ping command. So if you’re trying to figure out how a particular command works, all you need to do is type in a man, space, and then the command name that will open a manual page for you where you can read about whatever you want to know about that command. Nowadays, in the Internet age, it’s much easier to do a simple Google search than to try to do it with the man-page. However, it’s nice to have the man pages handy when Internet access is down. The man pages in Windows are basically the old “question mark” command where the “Help” pages are shown to you. The only downside about Linux’s Man pages is that it doesn’t explain much to you. It doesn’t necessarily make things straightforward. So when you go to the pages of the guy and look up the ping button, you will get all this text page that will tell you about the ping order. However, you do not know how to get out of the page until you are there. It is not simple and straightforward to use. You can hit the key to Escape, but that will not do anything. You can try clicking Enter or Backspace, but they do not do anything either. You need to type in the letter “Q” for quit to exit the man pages. You will drop out of the man pages when you type the letter “Q” So unless you press and experiment with every key in your keyboard, you won’t find out that the command to exit from the man page is by typing the letter “Q.” As you can see, this is counter-intuitive because most people are used to pressing the Windows Escape key to exit from anything. So now, let us tell you to want to know about the ping button. Go ahead and enter the instruction underneath:$Ping ManAfter typing in and pressing ENTER, you’ll see the ping command man page just like the one below:![](image_rsrc8P6.jpg)Looking at the ping command man page, you can see the overview of the command, and also the different choices associated with the ping order. The options are basically suffixes of the ping command, which further enhances what the basic ping command can do. One example of this is to use the “-t” suffix after the ping command to ping a specific host continuously. Now let us say you want to know about the apt-get command. Go ahead and type the command below: $man apt-get Once you type it in and press ENTER, you get the apt-get command man page like the one below:![](image_rsrc8P7.jpg)And now, this man page for the apt-get command will tell you everything you need to know about the apt-get command. Like what we have mentioned earlier, some of the man pages for these commands are about as long as a whole book. Remember, if you want to get out of the man page for any reason at all, just press Q on your keyboard to exit.TaskselThe next order we will be talking about is the tasksel. This is “job pick.” Ubuntu is now seeking to become the top Linux delivery service. They did a lot of work to help develop Ubuntu Linux, and they know how to make it as user friendly as possible. One of the items they came up with was this software called tasksel or task pick, with that in mind. For example, when you’re attempting to set up an Apache web server, an email server, or a virtualization server, note that each of these servers requires a number of different programs to function. And if you’re trying to set up a web server, you need to install Apache, install MySQL, install PHP, and add the connectors to work together. The Ubuntu creators then figured that they could create a script that would just install all those things for you if you need to install them. So what’s going to happen is, if you know what the server is going to do and you’re not really concerned about having it extremely personalized, you can run the tasksel command and pick and sort of pre-packaged server you’d like to be. Ubuntu’s developers have come up with about ten to twenty separate server installation packages from which you could select. And if you run the tasksel button, what happens is that you will get a DOS screen where all these server installation packages are identified. For e.g., if you want to make your server an email server, all you need to do is pick an Email server and then install all the software you need for that specific form of server. For a trouble-free installation, this facilitates. It’s fast and user-friendly. With this order, you will have a full Apache Web server running in 30 minutes or less. The main thing you need to remember with this command is to put sudo at the front of the command. You might wonder, how come when we executed the man command we didn’t put sudo? Ok, when executed, the man order doesn’t automatically face any security risk. So it is one of those commands where sudo is not needed. However, with the tasksel button, note that we are using this function to load applications. Installation involves alteration of certain operating system files to support a specific program. If someone has exposure from a hacker and executes the tasksel order to install viruses, you’re going to have really big issues. Ubuntu’s developers are learning that too. They worry about people downloading malware that isn’t meant to be installed on the server. So, if you’re trying to update applications, or if you’re performing some administrative function, you must place sudo before the order you’re trying to launch. With that in mind, you must type in the syntax below to execute the tasksel command properly:$Tasksel sudoOnce you type in the above command and press ENTER, you will get the following screen:![](image_rsrc8P8.jpg)And now, you are in the browser of the tasksel. If you look closely, you’ll see different options that you can choose from. These are all the various packages you can easily install onto the server. If you wish to use your server in cloud computing, you can select one of the available cloud computing options. If you want your server to be a DNS server, you can choose the DNS server and so on. Once the package of your choice has been highlighted, simply hit the spacebar on your keyboard. That will put an asterisk, and in some cases, a checkmark on the left side of the box, and you can then press ENTER. What’s going to happen when you press ENTER is that the system can now load all the program packages that are required to get things running. Depending on how good your machine is, the cycle will take a few minutes. You’ll then have a fully functional server once the process is complete.![](image_rsrc8P9.jpg)Apt-getNow we will discuss how you can install individual programs. The thing you need to remember about Linux is that it’s an open-source world. Most of the software is free, or you pay for it in strange ways such as service agreements and so forth. Anything in the Windows environment has to go through the activation procedures. So, whether you’re using Quickbooks, Adobe, or Microsoft software itself, everyone is worried about someone pirating or stealing a product. To prevent piracy of their software, they put in these insane activation procedures, which require you to have the CD with the correct CD key, etc. And then, after installing it with the right code, you have to go off and hit some activation server so that the software creators can verify your installation. They can create things named repositories in Linux. Repositories are places on the internet that only house thousands of Linux programs on thousands. So you can just go to that repository and install the application from there instead of having to have some kind of disk. You need to have a CD or DVD of the software in Windows or Mac OS. You get screwed if you lose that disk. All of the software, or a huge portion of the software, is sitting out in these repositories in Linux. You can only go and snatch apps from those libraries as long as you are linked to the internet. That is the fastest way to update Linux application applications. There are other ways to build software in Linux, and in the later pages, we’ll talk about them. But for now, since we are still discussing the basics, we’re only going to concentrate on downloading from repositories. So basically, there’s already a configuration file inside a Linux computer that tells you where the repositories are. This apt-get command will go out to the repository when you run the apt-get command, and it will get whatever program you want to get and install for you. To execute an apt-get command correctly, simply type in the following syntax: $ sudo apt-get install. Let’s presume you want to install the Apache2 program on your computer. Simply type in the following command: $sudo apt-get Apache2 updateAfter entering the above command and pressing ENTER, Linux will go out to the internet-based repository, find the Apache2 program, and then install it on your Linux computer. That is so simple. Now, let’s presume you agree you don’t like Apache2 and now you want to use another webserver. Well, the Apache2 uninstall command is: $sudo apt-get remove Apache2\. This will go into your Linux computer and uninstall Apache2. That is so simple. This is how much of the applications you’ll use for your Linux server is installed and uninstalled. When you get smarter and get more experience with Linux, you’ll start buying proprietary software in the field of Linux. You may purchase special backup devices or even a special protection app. Some of these types of programs might not be in the repositories, and you may need to go through various steps to install those programs. But this apt-get command will work for 99 percent of the programs that anyone ever installs for Linux. These repositories contain thousands upon thousands of Linux programs. It can be a little difficult to find out what programs you would like to use. If you don’t know about the best programs to have on your Linux machine, it’s best to do a Google search on it. There are plenty of recommended Linux programs that are great for beginners. Everyone is poking fun at the people behind Microsoft Windows because you already know by now, as they still have some alerts. You get at least ten updates for your program every third day of the week, or every month. Many people say those updates are evidence that Microsoft is crap. The reality, however, is that every single operating system or software needs to be updated from time to time. The same is true in Linux. So, once all the software you want is installed on your Linux server and you want to update it, all you need to do is use the upgrade command. See below for a syntax:Upgrade $sudo apt-getWhat the above command would do is to see if the repository’s software is older than the one on the site you have. If the program version you have is older than the repository version, it will bring down that information and ask you if you want to update your software or not. If you’re sayingYes, it will update all the software you’ve on your computer automatically. This is basically all you need to understand regarding the installation and maintenance of your software at this point.ServicesAs we described earlier, Linux is best for servers. When you turn on a Linux server, they will only go until, basically, the equipment burns out. Once you turn on a Linux system, you need to reboot it very few times. We are talking once a year when we say very few times, and that is already overkilled. However, you may need to restart that individual software or service when you enter and change your configuration files in the software that you have running on your server. Let’s assume you have Apache2 built on your Linux server, and some of the configuration files are modified. These configuration files will not be loaded until the Apache2 service is restarted. Despite the fact that the computer stays on all the time, you have to restart the services every once in a while just to make sure they are updated. What we’re going to see now is how service starts, stops, and restarts. Type in the syntax below with this:$USDO / etc / init.d / start $USDO / etc / init.d / stop $USDO / etc / init.d / stopSo, let’s say you’ve changed your server’s Apache2 configuration files, and you need to restart it.Just type: reboot $sudo /etc / init.d / Apache2This would restart the Apache2 service, bringing in whatever configuration changes you have made online. Now, let’s say you’re playing with the web server, you’re making some changes, and you don’t want anyone coming in from the outside world while you’re making the changes. So, you want to completely stop the services, which essentially makes the webserver go offline.All you do is stop: $sudo /etc / init.d / Apache2The command above stops web service. It doesn’t stop anything else, however. This means that while no one from outside can access the website you are hosting, you can still make changes to the configuration files, edit options, modify settings, etc. At this point, only the actual component of the Web server is not working. Now let’s go ahead and see how this looks when you actually run it on Linux’s command prompt![](image_rsrc8PA.jpg)So, right now, we’re at our Linux server operating system’s command prompt or console. Let us assume in this example that you have Apache2 installed on your system, and you want to stop that service. The first thing you can do is type in the word sudo, then put a space after the sudoAfter that, /etc / init.d / Apache2 put a space again, then the word stop. Once you hit ENTER after typing in the above command, you will get the following screen: As you can see in the above screen, Linux has successfully stopped the service. The domain that is hosting this web server is not accessible over the internet at this point. The website is Down at the moment. To start the service, all you need to do is type in the same command we typed when the service was stopped, except to type start instead of stop at the end.![](image_rsrc8PB.jpg)When restarting a service—starting and stopping a service automatically—simply enter the same command. The only exception is instead of typing start or stops at the end, and you type restart. Again, what is incredibly nice with this in Linux is that you do not have to restart the entire server. If you restart an entire server, no matter how fast the operating system is, it is going to take a couple of minutes before everything is online. If you only have to restart the service, everything will be back online in seconds.![](image_rsrc8PC.jpg)TopThe next thing we need to explore is the order called top. If you come from the Windows world, basically think of the top command as your task manager. So, if you go to the prompt and type in top of the Linux command, what happens is that you will see how much your memory is being used, how much of your CPU is being used, and then all the processes currently running on your system. You will see something called the processor ID or PID, the use of the CPU per process, the use of memory per process, how long the process has been running, and so on. Thus basically, the top command is the Linux operating system’s task manager. If a process is running in the Windows task manager and you need to terminate it, you can just use your mouse to right-click and do the End process. In Linux with top, you just need to type in the syntax below if you need to terminate or kill a particular process: $K So as you can see from the above command syntax, all you need to do is type in letter K and the process ID number. So if, for example, you see process 1578 doing something stupid, you type in: $K 1578 Entering that command in will kill that particular process. All the Linux processes get their own specific ID number for the process. And if one particular process does something, it is not meant to do, and it is incredibly convenient for the user to define and terminate the process via the PID. Remember, the command kill or terminate will only work inside the top. The kill / terminate command won’t work on its own if you’re not in the top task manager. If the specific process you are attempting to destroy is something that is necessary for the Linux server to function properly, it will inform you that the process can not be killed/ended. If that’s the case, you might need to reboot your “once a year” to kill the process. So all you need to do to see the Top Task Manager is type in the command below: $sudo top Once you type in, you will have the screen below:![](image_rsrc8PD.jpg)The illustration above shows how he looks like the top task manager. If you look carefully, you can see what the computer’s uptime is, the total amount of operations, the current use of the CPU, ram use, switch files buffers, etc. That effectively provides you the same knowledge that a task manager would have in the operating environment of Microsoft Windows. You can see the process ID 1489 at the top right now, and the user is core. And if you look to the far right of that, you’ll see that process ID 1489 for the top task manager process actually is. You can also see the usage of the CPU, the use of the memory, and the time the top process is up and running. Now, if you need to understand how to better use the top command, you can type in the letter “H.” Type in letter H will show you the various commands you can use with the top command. What you see after typing H is essentially the top command aid page. It shows all the sub-commands in the top setting that you can use. To return to the top main environment, simply press any key. Now, let s say you want a process killed/ended. Let’s say you’re going to kill the top task manager to get out of it. So again, the method ID for the top is 1489, as you can see. First, type in K, and then ask which PID you want to kill.![](image_rsrc8PE.jpg)Once it asks you which PID to kill, type 1489, and then press the ENTER key, it will then ask you to re-verify that you want to kill process 1489.![](image_rsrc8PF.jpg)Go ahead and press ENTER again to terminate the top task manager and bring you back to the Linux command prompt. It’s that simple.ConclusionI hope that this book has helped you learn how to use Linux confidently, from installing it into your computer to creating your own programs using this operating system.I also hope that this book has served as a guide in choosing the best Linux distribution for your needs, as well as applications that will help you perform daily computing tasks. By the end of this book, you should have also learned how to operate within Linux environment using the command line and have managed to learn some steps in making your system secure.While not as popular as Windows—at least, for some—Linux is definitely one of the most reliable Operating Systems around—and the best part about it is that it’s free, so you don’t really have to pay for anything just to get it, and you also wouldn’t have to go for counterfeit types of Operating Systems just because you could not pay for the legal copy.The next step is to learn more about creating and editing shell scripts and create automation scripts that will allow you to use Linux in a more efficient manner.Book 3: Learn python programmingLearn about python language, data analysis and algorithms and wi level up your skills in computer programming to become an expert PythonistaIntroductionWhy was Python developed?The reason why Guido Van Rossum embarked on the move to design a new programming language is that he wanted a language that could offer a simple syntax just like the ABC. This motivation led to the development of a new language named Python.But you may be wondering why just the name Python?First, this language wasn’t named after the huge snake called python. No! One of the interests of Rossum was watching comedy. He was a great fan of the comedy series in the late seventies. As a result, the name of the language was borrowed from the “Monty Python’s Flying Circus.”Properties of Python

*   Easy to learn – The syntax of Python is simple and beautiful. Additionally, Python programmers enjoy writing its syntax than other languages. Python simplifies the art of programming and allows the developer to concentrate on the solution instead of the syntax. For a newbie, this is a great choice to start your Python career.
*   Portability – When it comes to Python portability, it offers you the ability to run Python on different platforms without making any changes.
*   Python is described as a high-level language – In other words, you don’t need to be scared of tedious tasks such as memory management and so on. Alternatively, whenever you execute a Python code, it will automatically change the language to a language that your computer understands. No need to be worried about any lower-level operations.
*   Object-oriented – Since it is an object-oriented language, it will allow you to compute solutions for the most difficult problems. Object-Oriented Programming makes it possible to divide a large problem into smaller parts by building objects. 
*   Has a huge standard library to compute common tasks – Python has different standard libraries for the programmer to use. As a result, you will not write all the lines of code yourself. Instead, you will only import the library of the relevant code.

Python is a multi-purpose language created by Guido van Rossum.  The language boasts of a simple syntax that makes it easy for a new learner to understand and use. More so, you don’t need to end a line with a semicolon. However, Python demands that you adhere to good practices like the right indentation. These little things simplify learning Python for beginners.The Language is ExpressivePython will let you write powerful programs that have many functions using fewer lines of code.Lovely Community and SupportThere is a large community of Python developers. When you write code that is easy to read, it simplifies the process of updating the software. Compatible with Key Platforms and SystemsAs said before, Python can run in different operating systems. You can even decide to use Python Interprets to run the code on a defined platform. Additionally, Python is an interpreted programming language. In other words, you can run the same piece of code on different platforms without recompiling. Therefore, you don’t need to recompile the code once you make any changes. In fact, you can run the altered code without recompiling and confirm the effect of changes done to the code instantly. This feature allows developers to change the code without increasing the building time.Many Open Source Technologies and FrameworksSince Python is an open source language, it helps save the cost of the software. Furthermore, the software application can be developed directly from the prototype by refactoring the python code. Still, Python simplifies coding and testing through the use of TDD methodology. The process is simple, but we do need to look at a few things first and learn some of the basics so that you can start to write your own Python code in no time. The basics you should know about PythonAs you get started with writing your own codes in this language, you will start to see that there are many parts that come with them. Being able to understand how these basic parts work will help you to write the codes that are needed in your program. This section will quickly go over some of the basics that you need to know before you start working with Python. The keywordsFirst, we need to take a look at these important keywords in the Python language. Similar to what you will find in other coding languages there is a list of keywords in Python that are meant to tell your text editor what to do. These keywords are reserved, and you should only use them for their intended purposes if you want to avoid any issues with your code writing. They are basically the commands that will tell your compiler how to behave, and they have to remain in reserve so that you can execute the code without unnecessary issues complicating the process.  How to name the identifiersOnce you are ready to write your own codes in Python, there are a few parts of the code you will encounter, and they are called ‘identifiers.’ These ‘identifiers’ go by a lot of different names so it can get a bit confusing, but they will help by getting your code to run the way you want it. You will notice that some of the names that we used throughout this guidebook for identifiers include functions, classes, variables, and entities. There are some rules to follow when naming your identifier but these rules will be the same no matter what type of identifier you are currently working with. Some of the rules that you should know about include:Naming these identifiers is simple. You can use both the lower-case and the upper-case letters any time that you would like. Numbers and the underscore symbol are allowed as well. Any combination of the above are allowed, just make sure that you aren’t adding in spaces in this name. So, you would name it ‘MyPythonProgram’ rather than My Python Program.The identifiers can't start with any numbers. It is fine to use numbers throughout the name, but you should never have this at the beginning of the name or in the first character at least. This will result in an error when you type it into the compiler so if you are uncertain about why you see an error, be sure to double-check if this is the reason.The identifier shouldn’t have one of the keywords that we talked about before. Doing this will confuse your compiler so just avoid it completely.As you can see these rules are not too complicated to keep in mind and outside of them, you can name your identifiers whatever you would like. Now, if you are working on the code and you forget one of the rules and give your identifier a name that is not allowed then the compiler will provide you with a syntax error which indicates that you need to go and fix it. StatementsYou will use a lot of ‘statements’ when writing out a code in Python. These are useful for your code because they are just strings of code, these include some of the other parts we have talked about, that you will send over to the compiler to execute. You can write out pretty much any type of statement that you would like in your code, as long as it is written out in a way that your compiler is going to understand. You can choose to make a statement short, such as having a few lines with it, or make it longer, there really aren't many rules that dictate how you should do it. CommentsThe next component that you will use quite a bit in making your own code is the ‘comments.’ These comments are helpful because if someone else is taking a look through the code that you wrote and they need some clarification on what exactly it is you are talking about. You can write out the comments so that others who want to read through or use the code will know what is going on. They are basically little notes inside the code that help you and other programmers to know what is happening during the application.It is pretty easy to write out the comments that you want to add to your code. You simply need to use the (#) sign in front of the comment that you want to write. So, you could write out something like #this is my Python code. When the comment is done, you just hit return and start writing the rest of the code on the next line. As long as you have that sign right in front of the comment, then your compiler can just skip over it and will not read out what you put in the comment. You have the freedom to choose how many comments you would like to leave inside of your code. Sometimes the code will just need a few of them while other times the code may need a lot of comments. Keep the comments down to the ones that you really need and do not waste time or space writing out more comments than needed.  Functions‘Functions’ are another important part of the Python programming language. This is basically a part of the code that you can reuse, and it is often used to finish off one action in the code. Functions are great because they are more effective than other options, which means you can work on your code without wasting a lot of time. There are a lot of functions that are found in the Python language so you can benefit from this, plus, you are able to write out some of your own functions as well.   While you are working on your code, you have to make sure that you are defining the function. Once you are able to define the function and it is considered finalized, it is time to execute it to work in the code. You have the choice to call it up from the Python prompt, or you can call it up from another function.When this is placed into your compiler, you will see the two statements that we wrote out inside of the code that will come up just like a message. You would call up a function, and you can always change up the statements that are inside the code to figure out how you want to execute them later.  Working with variablesAnd finally, it is important that you spend some time to focus on variables. Variables are important because they will save up spots on your computer's memory to hold onto parts of your code. When creating a new variable, in effect, you are making sure that some of the space on your computer will be reserved. In some cases, such as when you are working with data types, your interpreter will do the work of deciding where this information should be stored and this helps speed up the process.  When it comes to working with variables, your job is to simply make sure that the right variables are lining up with the right values. This will ensure that the right parts show up in your code at the right time. The good news is that you are able to give the variable any value that you would like, but do check if it actually works inside of your code first. When you are ready to assign a new value to a variable, you can use the equal sign to make this happen.Chapter 2\. How to install pythonGuide to install Python on your Windows computer, Linux, and Mac OS X.How to Install Python in Mac OS X

1.  Navigate to the Python Download page on the official website and click “Download Python” (There will be a version name at the end.)
2.  Once the download completes, open the package and begin to follow the instructions. You will then see the notification “The installation was successful” when Python completes the installation successfully.
3.  It is advised to download a great text editor before you can start the installation process. For new beginners, you should download the Sublime Text. It is available for free.
4.  The installation process of Sublime text is quite simple.
5.  Write the code and save it. For newbies, here’s a code to use:

Print (“Hello, World!”)The simple program prints, “Hello, World!”

7.  Navigate to Tool > Build. Here, you can move to the bottom to see the results of your program.
