As you can see, we can run our programs with the command python trial.py. Under normal circumstances, this is the preferred way of running the program. Well, but is there a way to run Python programs without adding a python command to the top? Here comes the importance of the #! / Usr / bin / env python line we just mentioned. In order to run our program without introducing a python command, we first need to authorize it. We do this with the help of the following command:First: cd Desktop command where we save the file to the desktop. After that: chmod a + x test.py command, we give the permission to run the file named test.py, that is, we make the file executable. Now we can run our program by running the following command on the command line: ./ experiment.py So, what does all of this have to do with the #! / Usr / bin / env python line? If we don't put this line in the text, The. / test.py command will not work because the system cannot understand which program to run this script. When we run python test.py, it is understood that the Python program will run the script. However, when we remove the leading python statement, if #! / Usr / bin / env python does not exist in the first line of the script, the system cannot decide what to do with it. If you type this line as #! / Usr / bin / python and if the Python executable file is not in the / usr / bin / directory on the system where your program is running, you will get an error output like: bash: ./ trial.py: / usr / bin / python: bad interpreter: No such file or directory In order to avoid such an error, we try to write that line in the form of #! / usr / bin / env python. So no matter where Python is installed, our system can detect Python. The py command will not work. What if we want to run our program just by typing its name? In order to do this, our program must be included in the PATH variable that is to say in Turkish, the program must be on the PATH. What does it mean when a program is on the WAY? As is known, the path to a program or file is roughly the directory in which that program or file is located. For example, the path to the fstab file is / etc / fstab. To give another example, the path to the xorg.conf file is /etc/x11/xorg.conf. This road also has a more specific meaning. In the computer language, the executable files (.bin and .bin). sh files are executable files) in which the paths are specifically named PATH, and when used in this sense, the path word is usually capitalized. When we call a program, the operating systems look in some special directories to run the program. If the executable files are in these special directories, the operating system finds them and makes them run. So we can call these programs directly by name. Now let's do a few experiments to understand this issue better. In the computer language, executable files (. exe files are executable files) and the paths in which they are used are referred to as paths. When we call a program, the operating systems look in some special directories to run the program. If the executable files are in these special directories, the operating system finds them and makes them run. So we can call these programs directly by name. Now let's do a few experiments to better understand this issue. Let's start MS-DOS followed by Start / Run, command cmd in the pop-up window and write a command like this: echo% PATH% The output of this command will look something like this: C: \ WINDOWS \ system32; C: \ WINDOWS; C : \ WINDOWS \ system32 \ Wbem; This output shows what we call the PATH variable in English. When we want to call a program, our operating system will first check inside the directories named above. Therefore, if you want to add a new directory to the PATH, you must separate it from the previous directory with a semicolon. For example, notepad.exe is a program located under the C: \ Windows directory. Since this directory is located on the PATH, we can call Notepad directly by name. This variable holds extensions that the system considers executable. Echo% PATHEXT% Here we get the following output: .com; .exe; .bat; .cmd; .vbs; .vbe; .js; .jse; .wsf; .wsh if it has one, it will be considered executable. As you can see, .exe is one of these extensions. Therefore, we can call a file with this extension without specifying its extension. You can also add the extension to this list if you want. To do this, you can: 1\. Double-click the System icon in the Control Panel. 2\. Enter the Advanced tab and press the Environment Variables button. 3\. Introduction to data analysisData analysis includes all the techniques and processes used in extracting information from raw data. Since raw data is usually unstructured in form, and hardly informative, the need to organize such data becomes even more important. While there are many extra tools that can be used for handling data analysis i.e. Microsoft Excel, R-language, SQL, etc., most data scientist prefer to use Python due to its extensive libraries and support packages for data analysis. The most popular packages/frameworks which we would be exploring in this chapter are NumPy and Pandas. To risk over-simplification, data science is just the application of various techniques – usually employing the fast and efficient data organization, visualization and interpretation of computer programs or software to transform raw data into information for decision-making. Advertisements are better targeted at consumers, companies are more aware of their economic performance and possible trends or options in production, etc.  If you’re browsing the introductory pages of this book on a preview or something of the like, on the fence as to whether or not you want to learn Python, well, I can tell you that you probably should.As you continue to grow as a programmer after this book, you’re going to learn when you should and shouldn’t use Python just as a matter of intuition. Python is an absolutely fantastic language, but the place where it fails is when you have to get extremely close to a computer’s hardware or write incredibly efficient programs. In these areas, Python doesn’t excel.However, that’s not to say it doesn’t have its perks. In fact, I’d say that’s one of the few places that Python falls flat. And what it lacks there, it makes up for in other areas. For example, development time in Python is generally extremely low in comparison to other languages.However, without the right tools, guidance, and dedication, mastering the skills required for data science would be a very tedious and lengthy task. This is the reason for writing this book; to bring you up to speed on the skills and tools to begin a journey into the exciting and rewarding world of data science. At this point, you are probably quite excited about data science and the secrets it has to offer, however, you may ask “what does Python have to do with it?”Remember, data science leverages the exceptional processing and data manipulation capacity of computers? To do this, the data scientist must communicate the task in a clear and logical manner to the computer.Also, Python has an extensive library of tools and applications that makes data science less technical for beginners or intermediate programmers. Therefore, most data scientists have Python as their primary tool for big data, data analysis, etc. Chapter 10\. ConclusionFirst, Python is open source, cross platform and nearly ubiquitous.  In fact, if you are using a Mac or Linux system there is a good chance it is already installed on your computer.  To check, open up a terminal window and issue the following command:python –VIf python is installed you should get a response like:Python 2.6.1Which tells you the current version of Python installed on your system?  If it is not installed, a quick trip to http://www.python.org/downloads will give you download access to Python installers for a broad range of operating systems. For advanced users, the Python source code is also available from that location which allows for the modification and/or custom compiling of Python to suit specific applications.  Aside from having Python installed, all that is needed to start programming is a text editor.  The process can be streamlined and made easier with a Python friendly IDE, but it is not required.Next, Python is incredibly easy to use and extremely powerful.  While Python code can be compiled and packaged into an executable, it is typically used as an interpreted scripting language. As such, housekeeping chores such as memory management are handled by the interpreter, freeing the programmer to focus on function. Additionally, the interpreter is compiled for the operating system it is installed on so Python programs can be fairly system independent.  In other words, code written on a Mac will in many cases run fine on a Windows computer, or even a Raspberry Pi. Python also uses very simple 'readable' syntax which is similar in format to other languages.  Someone new to programming can be creating simple programs within a few minutes.  Those with programming experience in other modern languages (like C/C++) will find the syntax familiar, facilitating a quick jump into more complex tasks.Finally, all the benefits offered by Python have made it one of the most popular programming languages in use today.  That broad user base coupled with its open source nature translates to a huge, easily accessible resource pool. Resources from application specific libraries to online tutorials, online forums, and even college courses are never far away.  This level of community support is invaluable to users from the novice level to top tier pros.Whether your task is to sort incoming emails on a server, or build a control system for a revolutionary deep sea drone, Python provides an easy to learn open source solution with unparalleled community support to get the job done quickly and efficiently.SQL  PROGRAMMING         AND CODINGLEARN THE SQL LANGUAGE USED BY APPS AND ORGANIZATIONS, HOW TO ADD, REMOVE AND UPDATE DATA AND LEARN MORE ABOUT COMPUTER PROGRAMMING[Michael Learn]Text Copyright © [Michael Learn]
